Getting ready for autonomous mobility 
Robotaxis are arriving in Europe , but r egulatory gates are opening gradually . Berlin's autonomous bus pilot 
alone spans a 2023 -2027 runway from planning to launch, with authorization landing only mid -2027, while China 
and the US have already built a 100+ city blueprint to borrow from. The bigger brake may be cultural: European 
approval of autonomous vehicles sits near 33%, against roughly 60% in China. That gap is narrowing, but slowly, 
and 2027 -2028 will s tay experimental rather than commercial . Even generously scaled, Europe's robotaxi fleet 
reaches only 300-600k vehicles by 2035, half the US, a third of China's, and capturing at best 17% of global volume 
in a bull case, 7% in base. Europe's approval architecture is consumer -protection-first, business-case second, and it 
shows in the timeline. Each extra approval layer compresses the payback window on capex -heavy hardware, and 
that asymmetry is precisely what deters a European manufacturing play: no domestic tech champion exists, entry 
costs are steep and the regulatory calendar guarantees Europe will always scale second.

Robotaxis are the headline, but not the biggest or the smartest door into autonomy. Autonomous trucking is set 
to scale faster and bigger than robotaxis , a projected five-year CAGR of roughly +85% versus +50% for robotaxi s, 
with the truck segment reaching close to USD550bn by 2035 against robotaxi's USD420bn, while L3/L4 technology 
is still expected to represent under 9% of new car registrations that same year. That's a small, capital -intensive, 
already-concentrated market: China and the US dominate deployment  and the upfront fleet, and software costs 
are steep. A European OEM entering now would be underwriting high risk for thin, late-mover reward. The smarter 
play is backing adoption, not owning it , by partnering with established tech providers to secure distribution rights 
and data access across a market that will fragment into multiple channels (freight, transit, passenger) rather than 
building proprietary stacks from scratch. Robotaxis ’ real value to Europe are as a technology bridge: the same 
sensing and routing layers proven there translate directly into autonomous public transport, which aligns far better

with the continent's own agenda (i.e. decarbonizing cities, easing urban congestion and extending mobility services 
to an aging population etc.). Partnership is how Europe can capture the technology without paying the first-mover 
bill. 
Figure 22 & 23: Estimated global market size of robotaxis and autonomous trucks (in USD bn) / estimated market 
share of new global car registration per autonomous technology level breakdown

Sources:  Goldman Sachs, WEF, Allianz Research  
Firms should  invest for the next decade, not the next earnings call.  Short-termism is the enemy of disruptive 
technology: Autonomous mobility demands heavy upfront capital with payback horizons that don't fit a quarterly 
mindset. The temptation is to chase whichever segment looks hottest today and retreat at the first cost overrun , 
exactly the pattern that made Cariad expensive and robotaxi economics forbidding. The discipline needed is the 
opposite: identify which autonomous layers compound in value over 10 years, commit capital in stages tied to proof 
points and resist the urge to fund flashy pilots that don't scale into durable operational channels. 
A tactical and timely shift toward defense 
Defense diversification is the elephant in the piece, but not yet plainly assumed by carmakers.  Defense 
diversification remains the unspoken opportunity for European carmakers , but the window to act is narrowing. As 
EU defense budgets surpass 2% of GDP, with frontrunners like Poland exceeding 4%, automakers are quietly 
exploring adjacencies in the defense sector. The industrial logic is compelling: Advanced mechanical engineering, 
scalable manufacturing capacity, and overlapping supply chains position them naturally to address surging 
demand for unarmored vehicles, armored platforms and UAV components . These are precisely  categories where 
volumes have accelerated sharply since the onset of the Russia-Ukraine conflict. Historically, European automakers 
have pivoted to defense production during periods of geopolitical stress, making this convergence neither new nor 
far-fetched. The timing is uniquely favorable: defense primes face capacity bottlenecks despite record backlogs and 
are integrating AI -driven autonomous systems, while automakers urgently need to absorb excess capacity and 
amortize R&D in autonomous driving. On paper, this is a textbook win-win. Yet tangible P&L impact remains absent. 
Carmakers should move beyond exploratory postures , through joint ventures, dedicated defense units or co -
development programs in order  to capture a share of the unprecedented public funding flowing into European 
defense. This is not about abandoning core business; it is about extending industrial horizons where growth is most 
visible today.

Capital discipline and capacity management are key 
The constraint is behavioral, not financial.  Auto capex is collapsing ( -20%, the sharpest contraction since 2018) 
even as non-auto European blue-chip capex expands by +10%, and European auto assembly plants ran near only 
60% utilization in 2023, a fixed-cost drag with no revenue offset. The sector isn't short of cash or ambition, as Cariad, 
battery megafactories  and robotaxi flirtations all show; it is short of a filter for where that capital and capacity 
should go. Closing this equation means fixing the allocation process itself, not raising more money. 
Cut platform complexity, and make discipline the filter.  European automakers are still running bloated multi -
platform ICE/hybrid/EV lineups that dilute scale economies on all three; with BEVs on track to dominate European 
registrations within the decade, the rational move is to consolidate platforms now rather than stagger the transition. 
Fewer, better-scaled EV architectures beat a wide portfolio hedging against a shift that's already underway. That 
same discipline needs to extend to capital allocation more broadly: harder capital-allocation frameworks, with kill-
criteria on projects before launch, smaller bets sized to realistic market share rather than blue -sky TAM and a bias 
toward partnership over proprietary build where the technology isn't core (software OS, autonomous stacks, battery 
chemistry).  
Stop absorbing idle capacity, redeploy it into defense.  Rather than mothball or subsidize underutilized plants, 
diversify output toward adjacent, high -demand sectors . Defense is the clearest fit, given sustained European 
rearmament budgets and long -dated procurement commitments. Precision manufacturing, electronics and 
systems-integration skills transfer directly, turning spare capacity into a second, less cyclical revenue stream rather 
than dead weight on the balance sheet.

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -2.62 điểm tương ứng -0.15%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Dệt may, Công nghệ và Máy móc là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất trong 
tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -13,987 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã HDB, PNJ và VJC; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-30 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng 1,267 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua nhưng đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -2,363 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 3,726 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -2,702 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam trải qua một tuần đi ngang giằng co, giảm nhẹ và đóng cửa ở 1.735 điểm 
với thanh khoản ở mức thấp, và nhà đầu tư nước ngoài bán ròng cả 5 phiên trong tuần. 
Thông tin vĩ mô tích cực với tăng trưởng GDP quý 3 đạt mức gần 10% là điểm sáng trong 
tuần qua, tuy đã không đủ nâng đỡ thị trường chung. Thay vào đó, tâm lý thị trường thận 
trọng trước số liệu lạm phát ở mức cao, đợt tăng giá xăng dầu trong nước và mặt bằng lãi 
suất trong nước chưa có dấu hiệu hạ nhiệt. Diễn biến tăng của giá dầu thế giới, và lợi suất 
trái phiếu Chính phủ Mỹ cũng là các yếu tố gây áp lực lên thị trường chung. Tâm lý thận 
trọng còn thể hiện qua áp lực bán giải chấp ở một số cổ phiếu riêng lẻ như PNJ, SHB..., dù 
một vài nhóm trụ như dầu khí, tiêu dùng có thời điểm đóng vai trò nâng đỡ chỉ số.

Trần Đức Anh 
Giám đốc Kinh tế Vĩ mô & Chiến lược thị trường 
anhtd@kbsec.com.vn 
07/10/2026

Triển vọng TTCK 4Q2026 
Tích lũy trong vùng nhiễu động

Chúng tôi dự báo vùng điểm hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm 2026 
xuống 1,800 đi ểm, giảm mạnh từ mức 1,950 đi ểm đưa ra trong báo cáo gần nhất. 
Vùng điểm này tương ứng:

- Tăng trưởng EPS 2026 so với cùng kỳ của thị trường chung ở mức 33% 
(tăng mạnh so với mức 25% của báo cáo gần nhất), chủ yếu nhờ kỳ vọng 
vào các chính sách thúc đẩy kinh tế của Chính phủ, và lợi nhuận tăng 
trưởng đột biến ở VIC (xem thêm phần Triển vọng hoạt động kinh doanh).

- Mức P/E hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm ở mức 13.2 (giảm 
nhẹ so với mức 13.5 thời điểm hiện tại - phản ánh quan điểm các yếu tố 
rủi ro sẽ tiếp tục duy trì). Đây là mức định giá thấp so với bình quân 5 năm 
gần nhất ở 15.1. Nếu loại bỏ yếu tố đột biến ở nhóm Vingroup, chỉ số P/E 
điều chỉnh hiện đã rơi xuống 10.9, thấp hơn các đáy gần nhất năm 2020, 
2022 và 2025. Trong bối cảnh kinh tế Việt Nam duy trì tốc độ tăng trưởng 
cao, lợi nhuận các doanh nghiệp niêm yết liên tục bứt phá, vùng định giá 
này mở ra cơ hội đầu tư trung/dài hạn. Chúng tôi cho rằng ngay khi các rủi 
ro vĩ mô quốc tế (xoay quanh vấn đề Iran), và trong nước (xoay quanh vấn 
đề lãi suất) hạ nhiệt, thị trường chung sẽ có cơ hội tái định giá lên mức cao 
hơn đáng kể so với thời điểm hiện tại. Dù vậy, khác với báo cáo gần nhất 
vào tháng 7, ở báo cáo này, chúng tôi đẩy lui thời điểm các yếu tố rủi ro vĩ 
mô hạ nhiệt sang năm 2027, đồng nghĩa với quan điểm thận trọng hơn về 
thị trường cho quý 4 2026.

Cụ thể, đối với vấn đề lãi suất, áp lực lạm phát lớn dần, đi kèm nhu cầu vốn cao phục 
vụ hoạt động đầu tư cơ sở hạ tầng sẽ khiến mặt bằng lãi suất duy trì ở mức cao cho 
phần còn lại của năm 2026 (dù thanh khoản hệ thống ghi nhận sự cải thiện đáng kể 
trong 1 quý gần đây). Đối với vấn đề xung đột Trung Đông, các cuộc đàm phán vẫn 
đang bế tắc và ít có kh ả năng dòng ch ảy dầu mỏ tại khu vực này được nối lại ngay 
trong năm nay.

Chủ đề đầu tư chúng tôi l ựa chọn cho 4Q2026 là đ ầu tư công, chính sách ti ền tệ hỗ 
trợ, và xung đột Iran.

Xem đầy đủ tại đây.

Chỉ báo  Nhận định xu hướng Hành động 
Xanh đậm  Khả năng tăng điểm cao Mua mạnh 
Xanh nhạt  Khả năng tăng điểm vừa phải Mua từng phần 
Vàng  Xu hướng trung tính Mua/Bán trading 
Cam  Khả năng giảm điểm vừa phải Bán từng phần 
Đỏ  Khả năng giảm điểm cao Bán mạnh

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành mẫu nến Doji trên khung đồ thị tuần sau hai tuần giảm điểm liên tiếp, 
cho thấy áp lực bán đã phần nào được kiểm soát. Trên khung ngày, nỗ lực hồi phục đầu 
tuần gặp lực cản tại đường SMA50 khiến VNINDEX quay đầu giảm điểm, tiếp tục chớm phá 
đáy ngắn hạn; tuy nhiên vùng hỗ trợ gần đã tạo hiệu ứng thu hút lực cầu bắt đáy, giúp chỉ 
số thu hẹp đà giảm trong phiên cuối tuần. Sau nhịp điều chỉnh khá tiêu cực từ vùng đỉnh 
đầu tháng 9, VNINDEX đang bước vào giai đoạn tìm kiếm điểm cân bằng trở lại và diễn biến 
giằng co điều chỉnh có thể còn kéo dài thêm trong các phiên kế tiếp. Tuy nhiên khi xu 
hướng tăng ngắn hạn tính từ vùng đáy cuối tháng 7 vẫn đang được bảo lưu, chỉ số được kỳ 
vọng sẽ có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại tại điểm đỡ gần quanh 1710 (đây cũng là chốt chặn cần 
giữ để bảo vệ được xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn). 
Kháng cự xa: 1820 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1775 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1710 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1670 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

### Page 2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX tăng điểm trong tuần qua, 20.45 điểm tương ứng 1.14%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Dầu khí, Bảo hiểm và Tiện ích khí là các nhóm ngành có mức tăng rõ nét nhất trong 
tuần. Khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị 2,680 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, HDB và BSR; đồng thời, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng ở 
mức 1,172 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại mua ròng 3,009 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua và đang 
duy trì vị thế mua lũy kế 5,001 hợp đồng. Tự doanh bán ròng -9,259 hợp đồng và đang duy 
trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng 9,458 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam có tuần tăng điểm tích cực, chỉ số VNINDEX chinh phục thành công 
ngưỡng tâm lý 1.800 điểm với thanh khoản cải thiện, độ rộng thị trường nghiêng về số mã 
tăng điểm và nhà đầu tư nước ngoài quay trở lại mua ròng. Động lực hỗ trợ thị trường 
trong tuần qua đến từ hoạt động cơ cấu của các quỹ bám theo rổ chỉ số mới nổi của FTSE, 
khi mà ngày 21/9 là ngày Việt Nam được chính thức nâng hạng. Thêm vào đó, diễn biến hạ 
nhiệt của giá dầu khi lo ngại thiếu hụt cung do xung đột ở Trung Đông giảm bớt cũng là 
yếu tố hỗ trợ tâm lý thị trường chung. Ở chiều ngược lại, thông tin FED tăng lãi suất có tác 
động tiêu cực nhưng ảnh hưởng không đáng kể lên thị trường do động thái này đã được 
dự báo từ trước.

### Page 3

3

Fed tăng lãi suất, dự báo thêm 
một lần tăng nữa trong năm 
Đánh giá áp lực lên tỷ giá và lãi suất Việt Nam

Fed tăng lãi suất và dự báo có thêm một lần tăng lãi suất nữa trong năm 
— Trong kỳ họp FOMC ngày 15–16/09/2026, Fed quyết định tăng 25bps lãi suất điều hành 
lên mức 3.75%–4%, đánh dấu lần tăng lãi suất đầu tiên kể từ năm 2023. Quyết định 
được thông qua với tỷ lệ đồng thuận 12–0, phản ánh sự thống nhất của các thành viên 
FOMC trong việc ưu tiên kiểm soát lạm phát. 
— Chủ tịch Fed Kevin Warsh nhấn mạnh lạm phát vẫn ở mức cao và cần được đưa về mục 
tiêu 2% trong thời gian phù hợp hơn. Bên cạnh đó, Fed đánh giá hoạt động kinh tế 
đang tăng trưởng vững, tiêu dùng nội địa duy trì tích cực, năng suất và đầu tư vốn tăng 
trưởng mạnh, trong khi thị trường lao động vẫn ổn định. Điều này cho thấy Fed chưa 
nhận thấy áp lực suy giảm kinh tế đủ lớn để ưu tiên nới lỏng chính sách tiền tệ.  Ông 
Warsh không đưa ra cam kết cụ thể về thời điểm tăng lãi suất, nhấn mạnh chính sách 
sẽ phụ thuộc vào diễn biến lạm phát, thị trường lao động và các dữ liệu kinh tế sắp tới. 
— Biểu đồ dot plot tháng 9 cho thấy Fed vẫn nghiêng về khả năng tăng thêm lãi suất 
trong năm 2026, với 16/18 thành viên dự báo ít nhất một lần tăng nữa, đưa lãi suất điều 
hành lên vùng 4%–4.25%. Tuy nhiên, sự phân hóa trong dự báo lãi suất năm 2027 
(3.625%–4.375%) cho thấy đây có thể là một lần điều chỉnh nhằm kiểm soát lạm phát, 
thay vì tín hiệu về một chu kỳ thắt chặt kéo dài.

Quan điểm của KBSV đối với lãi suất và tỷ giá 
— Trong 3 tháng cuối năm, chúng tôi đánh giá nhiều khả năng Fed sẽ có thêm một lần 
tăng lãi suất 25bps, dựa trên:  
(1) Fed tiếp tục nâng dự báo PCE headline và core 2026 lên thêm 10 bps so với dự 
báo hồi tháng 6, đồng thời dự báo lạm phát sẽ quay về mức mục tiêu 2% vào năm 
2029 (thay vì năm 2028 như dự báo trước đó), trong khi dự báo về tỷ lệ thất 
nghiệp và tăng trưởng GDP được điều chỉnh theo hướng tích cực hơn. 
(2) Giá dầu vẫn duy trì ở mức cao (dự kiến quay về vùng 9x USD/thùng vào quý 4) 
trong bối cảnh xung đột tại Trung Đông tiếp tục gây gián đoạn hoạt động vận 
chuyển và xuất khẩu dầu mỏ. Mặc dù Saudi Arabia đã tăng cường vận chuyển 
dầu qua Oman, qua đó giảm bớt lo ngại về thiếu hụt nguồn cung trong ngắn hạn, 
rủi ro gián đoạn vẫn hiện hữu khi xung đột chưa được giải quyết hoàn toàn. 
— Chúng tôi nâng dự báo DXY lên vùng 98–103 điểm trong phần còn lại của năm với động 
lực hỗ trợ USD trong ngắn hạn đến từ việc Fed phát tín hiệu tiếp tục thắt chặt, trong khi 
thị trường điều chỉnh kỳ vọng lãi suất theo hướng duy trì cao hơn. Tuy nhiên, việc các 
NHTW lớn khác cũng duy trì định hướng kiểm soát lạm phát có thể hạn chế mức độ mở 
rộng chênh lệch lãi suất, qua đó làm giảm khả năng USD tăng giá một chiều và DXY 
tăng mạnh khỏi vùng dự báo.

Kết quả kinh doanh 2Q2026 tích cực 
nhờ hưởng lợi từ căng thẳng địa 
chính trị ở Trung Đông

Giá dầu duy trì ở mức cao, tiếp tục hỗ 
trợ biên lợi nhuận khí khô của GAS

Luật Dầu khí sửa đổi mở rộng dư địa 
phát triển mỏ cận biên, tạo động lực 
tăng nguồn khí cho GAS trong dài 
hạn

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
96,300 VND/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu Q2/2026 tăng trưởng tích cực 44% YoY, chủ yếu nhờ (1) giá bán khí tăng 
và (2) tăng trưởng sản lượng tích cực từ mảng LNG. Trong khi đó, LNST-CĐTS tăng 
chậm hơn, ở mức 23% YoY, do ghi nhận khoản hoàn nhập dự phòng nợ phải thu khó 
đòi thấp hơn so với cùng kỳ năm trước.

Giá dầu Brent tăng trở lại lên vùng 95 USD/thùng trong bối cảnh xung đột Mỹ - Iran 
tiếp tục leo thang, làm gián đoạn nguồn cung dầu tại Trung Đông, trong khi lưu 
lượng tàu qua Eo biển Hormuz vẫn ở mức thấp và tồn kho dầu toàn cầu cùng Kho 
Dự trữ Chiến lược Mỹ liên tục suy giảm. Chúng tôi kỳ vọng giá dầu duy trì vùng 85 - 
95 USD/thùng trong ngắn hạn, qua đó tiếp tục hỗ trợ biên lợi nhuận mảng khí 
thượng nguồn của GAS nhờ chênh lệch giữa giá bán khí theo giá dầu và giá miệng 
giếng cố định tại các mỏ lâu đời.

Luật Dầu khí sửa đổi tăng ưu đãi cho các mỏ cận biên, giúp cải thiện hiệu quả đầu 
tư và thúc đẩy phát triển nguồn khí mới. GAS có thể hưởng lợi từ sản lượng khí bổ 
sung qua hệ thống hạ tầng hiện hữu, nhưng tác động chủ yếu trong trung và dài 
hạn.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp FCFF và EV/EBITDA để định giá cổ phiếu GAS, chúng tôi đưa 
ra khuyến nghị MUA với mức giá mục tiêu 96,300 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức 
upside 16.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 11/09/2026.

Kết quả kinh doanh tích cực trong 
2Q2026

Giá urea hạ nhiệt, xuất khẩu tiếp tục 
là động lực dẫn dắt doanh thu chính 
của DCM trong 2H2026

Giá khí đầu vào DCM 2028–2030F 
tăng do bổ sung nguồn khí giá cao từ 
Nam Du – U Minh

Luật Dầu khí mới cải thiện triển vọng 
nguồn khí đầu vào cho DCM trong 
dài hạn

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
42,100 VND/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu Q2/2026 tăng 10% YoY, chủ yếu nhờ giá bán urea tăng trong bối cảnh thị 
trường chịu tác động từ các yếu tố địa chính trị. LNST-CĐTS tăng 36% YoY, được hỗ 
trợ bởi giá bán đầu ra tăng nhanh hơn chi phí khí đầu vào.

Giá urea thế giới sau khi tăng cao trong Q2/2026 đã điều chỉnh về vùng trước chiến 
tranh do nhu cầu suy yếu và nguồn cung Trung Quốc quay trở lại. Đối với DCM, xuất 
khẩu urea tiếp tục là động lực dẫn dắt doanh thu chính, trong khi giá và sản lượng 
urea nội địa được kỳ vọng đi ngang trong 2H2026 do đây không phải mùa cao điểm 
tiêu thụ trong nước.

Chúng tôi điều chỉnh tăng 10% dự báo giá khí đầu vào DCM giai đoạn 2028–2030F, 
phản ánh việc bổ sung nguồn khí Nam Du – U Minh từ Q4/2028. Giá khí Nam Du – U 
Minh dự kiến 11.56 USD/mmBTU, cao hơn ~35% so với các nguồn hiện hữu, tạo áp 
lực tăng chi phí đầu vào.

Luật Dầu khí sửa đổi sẽ rút ngắn quy trình phê duyệt FDP và bổ sung ưu đãi cho các 
mỏ khó khai thác, qua đó hỗ trợ đầu tư E&P và duy trì sản lượng dầu khí trong 
nước. Đối với DCM, tác động dự kiến mang tính gián tiếp và có độ trễ, chủ yếu thông 
qua cải thiện nguồn cung khí nội địa, chưa phải động lực thúc đẩy lợi nhuận đáng 
kể trong ngắn hạn.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp FCFF và EV/EBITDA định giá cổ phiếu DCM chúng tôi đưa ra 
khuyến nghị MUA với mức giá mục tiêu 42,100 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức upside 
29.5% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 11/09/2026.

Doanh thu và LNST-CĐTS 2Q2026 
tiếp tục tăng trưởng tốt, được hỗ trợ 
bởi đà tăng của crack spread

Biên lợi nhuận duy trì ở mức cao 
trong ngắn hạn nhờ crack spread 
bùng nổ, nhưng có thể biến động 
mạnh

Biên lợi nhuận trung và dài hạn chịu 
áp lực từ rủi ro dư cung dầu

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
32,200 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Trong Q2/2026, doanh thu BSR tăng 60% YoY nhờ giá nhiên liệu tăng mạnh trong 
bối cảnh nguồn cung gián đoạn do xung đột Trung Đông. LNST-CĐTS tăng 807% 
YoY, được hỗ trợ bởi crack spread của bốn sản phẩm lọc dầu chủ lực tăng mạnh do 
nguồn cung thắt chặt.

Xung đột quân sự Mỹ – Iran gây gián đoạn nghiêm trọng dòng chảy dầu qua Eo biển 
Hormuz, thắt chặt nguồn cung dầu thô và thành phẩm, đẩy giá Brent lên quanh 100 
USD/thùng và đưa crack spread tại châu Á đạt đỉnh và duy trì ở vùng cao từ tháng 3 
đến nay. Diễn biến crack spread thời gian tới sẽ phụ thuộc đáng kể vào diễn biến 
chiến sự tại Eo biển Hormuz và tốc độ khôi phục lưu thông qua Eo biển này.

Triển vọng lợi nhuận trung và dài hạn của BSR chịu áp lực khi cán cân cung – cầu 
dầu toàn cầu được dự báo chuyển sang dư cung trong trung và dài hạn. Tuy nhiên, 
nhu cầu tái tích lũy tồn kho dầu toàn cầu có thể phần nào hấp thụ lượng cung dư 
thừa và hạn chế mức độ thu hẹp crack spread.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp FCFF và EV/EBITDA định giá cổ phiếu BSR chúng tôi đưa ra 
khuyến nghị Mua với mức giá mục tiêu 32,200 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức upside 
16.9% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 11/09/2026. Chúng tôi áp dụng mức EV/EBITDA mục 
tiêu là 15.8 lần, bằng với mức chuẩn trung bình 5 năm của cổ phiếu.

BÁO CÁO NGẮN CƠ HỘI ĐẦU TƯ

15/09/2026

CTCP Nhựa Tiền Phong (NTP) 
Tăng tốc trong chu kỳ thuận lợi

Mua 
Giá mục tiêu VND 73.200 
Vùng hỗ trợ/Điểm mua 1 50.000 – 51.400 
Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng 1 44% 
Vùng hỗ trợ/Điểm mua 2 42.000 – 44.000 
Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng 2 70%

Lưu ý: 
Nhà đầu tư cần kết hợp nhận định xu hướng thị 
trường chung, mức độ hấp dẫn của các điểm 
nhấn đầu tư và khẩu vị rủi ro của mình để quản 
lý tỷ trọng tại từng điểm mua cho phù hợp. 
Do việc xác định mỗi điểm mua được dựa trên 
các khung thời gian khác nhau nên chúng tôi 
không đưa ra mức dừng lỗ cố định. Nhìn chung, 
mức dừng lỗ nên được đặt chặt hơn cho điểm 
mua gần (5-7%) và rộng hơn cho điểm mua xa 
(10-15%).

ĐIỂM NHẤN ĐẦU TƯ 
Kỳ vọng sản lượng tiêu thụ tăng trưởng mạnh hơn so với giai đoạn trước. KBSV 
dự báo sản lượng tiêu thụ của NTP tăng 2,3% CAGR giai đoạn 2026 - 2028 nhờ (1) 
Đẩy mạnh giải ngân đầu tư hạ tầng tại miền Bắc (2) gia tăng đầu tư hạ tầng tại các 
địa phương thu hút FDI (3) tăng tốc đơn hàng mảng dự án.

Giá hạt nhựa sẽ gây áp lực biên lợi nhuận nửa cuối 2026 nhưng không làm thay 
đổi triển vọng dài hạn. Mặc dù duy trì chính sách dự phòng nguyên liệu cao và đa 
dạng hóa nhà cung cấp, nhưng NTP vẫn đối mặt với rủi ro thu hẹp lợi nhuận, 
nhưng sẽ không làm giảm đáng kể triển vọng tăng trưởng trung hạn. KBSV cho 
rằng tình trạng dư cung hạt nhựa tại Trung Quốc vẫn sẽ duy trì trong trung hạn và 
xung đột được giải quyết, giảm áp lực biên lợi nhuận toàn ngành.

Catalyst thoái vốn kỳ vọng tái định giá doanh nghiệp. Theo kế hoạch thoái vốn 
Nhà nước, SCIC dự kiến thoái toàn bộ 37,1% cổ phần tại NTP . Chúng tôi đánh giá 
việc hiện thực hóa thương vụ có thể giúp NTP (1) thu hút nhà đầu tư chiến lược 
mới; (2) cộng hưởng mô hình kinh doanh; (3) tái định giá doanh nghiệp.

Hoạt động kinh doanh  Diễn biến doanh thu và lợi nhuận thuần 5 năm gần nhất

NTP là một trong những doanh nghiệp đầu 
ngành ống nhựa tại Việt Nam, với thị phần 
khoảng 31% toàn quốc và 60% tại miền Bắc. 
Hoạt động kinh doanh cốt lõi tập trung vào 
sản xuất và phân phối ống nhựa cùng các sản 
phẩm phụ tùng. Nhờ lợi thế về thương hiệu, 
quy mô và mạng lưới phân phối, NTP duy trì 
vị thế dẫn đầu và nền tảng tăng trưởng ổn 
định qua các năm.

Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KB Securities Vietnam

Khuyến ngh ị: MUA – Giá m ục tiêu 
25,950 VNĐ/CP  
 Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng tăng trưởng tín d ụng 2026 đ ạt 27%, với động lực chính 
đến từ phân khúc KHDN, đ ặc biệt là t ừ nhóm FDI trong b ối cảnh dòng v ốn FDI vào 
Việt Nam và hoạt động xuất nhập khẩu duy trì tích cực. Xét về lĩnh vực, cho vay kinh 
doanh BĐS kỳ vọng sẽ tăng trưởng vượt trội khi ngân hàng tham gia vào thu xếp vốn 
cho các dự án hạ tầng và d ự án BĐS ưu tiên , trong khi các m ảng kinh doanh khác 
tăng trưởng ở mức khả quan.

Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng NIM giảm so với năm 2025 do áp l ực huy động khiến chi 
phí vốn duy trì ở mặt bằng cao, trong khi lãi su ất cho vay dự kiến ở mức cạnh tranh 
để ưu tiên mục tiêu tăng trưởng tín dụng.

Chúng tôi dự phóng tỷ lệ nợ xấu của ngân hàng sẽ tăng 11bps YoY trong bối cảnh lãi 
suất đang neo ở mức cao và kỳ vọng ngân hàng sẽ tiếp tục duy trì chính sách trích 
lập dự phòng thận trọng nhằm củng cố bộ đệm, qua đó hoàn thành mục tiêu duy 
trì tỷ lệ bao phủ nợ xấu ở mức trên 100%.

Dựa trên triển vọng kinh doanh của ngân hàng cũng như đánh giá các y ếu tố rủi ro, 
chúng tôi tiếp tục duy trì khuyến nghị MUA với cổ phiếu MBB với giá mục tiêu 25,950 
VNĐ/CP - tiềm năng tăng giá 26.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 17/09/2026.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX cho thấy xu hướng hồi phục trong tuần qua nhưng với diễn biến khá giằng co, 
được thể hiện qua sự hình thành của một mẫu nến "spinning" trên đồ thị tuần. Trên khung 
ngày, bức tranh có phần tích cực hơn khi chỉ số đã cho phản ứng với vùng hỗ trợ quanh 
1790 và hồi phục trở lại, phản ánh sự nhập cuộc khá chủ động của lực cầu. Vùng kháng cự 
gần quanh 1850 bắt đầu khiến áp lực bán có phần gia tăng trong phiên cuối tuần và diễn 
biến rung lắc có thể sẽ còn tiếp diễn. Mặc dù vậy, khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được 
bảo lưu, nhịp giảm điểm vừa qua nhiều khả năng chỉ là bước điều chỉnh tích lũy trước khi 
VNINDEX thử thách lại và có cơ hội vượt vùng đỉnh cũ.

Hỗ trợ xa: 1715 (+-15)

- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng.

- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

## Document 206: Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Consumer Checkpoint_ Strength in numbers September saw solid spending across many categories and a strong start to the holidays. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13026023     1

Economy 
Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers 
09 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Consumer spending remained very solid in September. Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), or 5.1% excluding gasoline, both the third strongest growth rates over the past four 
years. Month-over-month (MoM) spending was more muted, especially without gas. 
• Spending shows considerable breadth: the vast majority of discretionary retail and services categories are showing growth. 
Moreover, this strength extends across income cohorts, with the ongoing recovery of lower- and middle-income households' 
spending growth. 
• With the winter months fast approaching, holiday spending is already increasing at a solid rate, though it's still early. In Bank of 
America deposit data through September, buy now, pay later (BNPL) usage is rising but not at a rate that is overly concerning. 
However, nearly one in every five households that use BNPL has a repayment of over $500.

Consumer Checkpoint is a regular publication from Bank of America Institute. It aims to provide a holistic and real-time estimate of US 
consumers’ spending and their financial well-being, leveraging the depth and breadth of Bank of America proprietary data. Such data is not 
intended to be reflective or indicative of, and should not be relied upon as, the results of operations, financial conditions or performance of 
Bank of America. 
A solid September for spending   
Consumer spending continued to look solid in September. Bank of America total aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), while spending excluding gasoline increased 5.1% YoY. Notably, both are the third 
strongest growth rates in the past four years. Seasonally-adjusted total card spending rose 0.2% month-over-month (MoM), 
while spending excluding gasoline was little changed. Overall, spending growth remains well above the 2025 average rate 
(Exhibit 1). 
Exhibit 1: Total card spending per household rose 6.2% YoY and 5.1% YoY excluding gasoline in September 
Total card spending growth per household, based on Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card data (monthly, MoM%, seasonally adjusted (SA)) and 
(monthly, YoY%, non-SA, right-hand side (rhs))

### Page 2

2 09 October 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

Most spending categories are growing  
One striking thing about recent card spending, in our view, is that it is well balanced across categories. For example, both 
spending on retail (excluding gasoline) and services grew strongly YoY in September, at 3.5% and 5.4%, respectively. Meanwhile, 
retail spending is showing notably better momentum than in 2025 (Exhibit 2).  
Exhibit 2: Spending is rising across retail and services 
Retail (excluding gasoline) and services card spending per household (monthly, seasonally adjusted, index January 2025 = 100)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Well balanced across “nice-to-have” and “need-to-have” retail categories  
Is the momentum in retail spending reflective of “nice-to-have” discretionary or “need-to-have” necessity spending? In retail, 
this is a nuanced question, as many categories sit in a gray area between “necessity” and “discretionary”. General merchandise 
stores, for example, sell both essentials like groceries as well as nice-to-have electronics or even jewelry.  
In Exhibit 3, we account for this by grouping retail spending into “more discretionary” (furniture, electronics, building materials, 
online retailers, clothing retailers and miscellaneous stores), “partly discretionary” (auto, health and personal care retailers, 
general merchandise) and “mostly necessity” (gas and groceries). Even within these categories the edges will be blurred, but the 
overall impression is that retail spending growth is widely distributed, with spending rising across all fronts. One caveat: “mostly 
discretionary” spending has been making a smaller contribution since June. 
Exhibit 3: Retail spending is distributed across discretionary and necessity categories  
Percentage points contribution to YoY growth by type of retail spending (three-month moving average)

balanced and broad-based (Exhibit 4). Non-store and online retailers and miscellaneous stores saw some of the fastest growth, 
while furniture and building materials were among the laggards, though other durable categories showed some strength.

For services, growth is driven mostly by discretionary spending  
In services spending, on the other hand, the boundaries between discretionary and necessity spending are often clearer. Travel 
spending (e.g., airlines, lodging) can be thought of as, by and large, discretionary. At the same time, utility bills, rent and 
insurance payments are necessities.  
And in services, discretionary spending remains the clear driver, accounting for over three-quarters of YoY spending growth in 
September (Exhibit 6). Leisure and housing services are major components of growth, though travel and restaurants also made 
positive contributions. However, as we noted last month strength in airline spending largely reflects price rises rather than 
transactions growth (read more in Consumer Checkpoint: Still sizzling).

Lower-income households’ spending outpaced all but the top 5% 
The breadth in spending growth across categories is also evident across income cohorts. In September, lower-income 
households’ total card spending growth was 5.5% YoY, while the equivalent figures for middle- and higher-income households 
were 4.9% and 4.8% YoY, respectively. The top 5% of households by income continue to show the fastest spending growth, 
5.9% in September, though this has eased somewhat (Exhibit 7). 
Overall, the divergences across income cohorts are much smaller than they were in 2025 and the first three months of this year. 
Just focusing on spending excluding key necessities (gasoline, food and utilities) doesn’t meaningfully change the picture of 
convergence across income cohorts (Exhibit 8).  
Exhibit 7: The top 5% saw the strongest spending growth of any 
income cohort…

The strength in lower- and middle-income households’ discretionary spending is occurring despite necessity spending shifting 
from a tailwind to a headwind over the past year amid rising gas prices (Exhibit 9). In our view, a key component of why these 
households have been able to combine rising necessity spending with strength in discretionary is the labor market and a 
recovery in their after-tax wage growth (Exhibit 10).  
In September, lower-income wage growth eased to 4.5% YoY from 4.7% YoY in August, while growth for middle- and higher-
income households rose to 3.8% and 3.7% YoY, respectively. These changes were modest, and lower-income households are 
continuing to see faster after-tax wage growth than other cohorts, a notable turnaround from the “K-shaped” pattern we 
observed over much of 2025 and 2026.

Should we be concerned about the easing in household spending and after-tax wage growth for those with the top 5% of 
incomes? For now, probably not, as in our view this spending growth is likely being driven by wealth effects rather than wage 
gains. Absent a sharp correction in equity markets, there is little reason to expect them to retrench their spending. However, 
developments here are worth watching as this cohort comprises a significant proportion of overall consumer spending. They also 
tend to make outsized contributions to several discretionary spending categories (Exhibit 11).  
Exhibit 11: The top 5% of households by income make up a disproportionate amount of spending in highly discretionary categories

With summer gone, thoughts turn to the holidays and BNPL… 
Now that summer is over, the next big gauge of consumer momentum is how people spend during the run-up to, and over, the 
holiday season. It is early days, but September spending on holiday items ran well ahead of 2025 (Exhibit 12). Given the backdrop 
of firmer after-tax wage growth and breadth across retail and services categories, this is perhaps not surprising. However, some 
of this spending strength could be due to rising prices as opposed to more purchases.

A concern often raised as we move into the holidays is whether consumers will lean on less traditional forms of credit such as 
buy now, pay later (BNPL) to finance their spending. Frequent BNPL users tend to have higher credit card utilization rates (read 
more in: Do consumers have wiggle room to absorb higher gas prices?).  
Looking at the share of households in Bank of America deposit data with a BNPL payment, we do tend to see a seasonal rise 
over the holidays (Exhibit 13), though in past years, these increases have quickly reversed. Further, while the underlying trend in 
BNPL usage is upwards, there are no signs of a sharp acceleration. We do, however, see some evidence that BNPL is being used 
to finance more expensive purchases, with the share of households making repayments above $500 rising, albeit relatively slowly 
(Exhibit 14).

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Store category aggregation for “more discretionary”, “partly discretionary”, and “mostly necessity” is defined as follows: 
More discretionary: furniture, electronics, building materials, hobbies, miscellaneous stores, nonstore retail, clothing. 
Partly discretionary: auto, health and personal care, general merchandise. 
Mostly necessity: Gasoline, food and beverage.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts. 
The “more discretionary” retail category includes furniture, electronics, building materials, online marketplaces, clothing retailers and 
miscellaneous stores; while “partly discretionary” retail contains auto parts and services, health and personal care retailers, general merchandise 
and “mostly necessity”  retail contains gas and groceries. 
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

## Document 235: First Impressions_ NZIER Quarterly Survey of Business Opinion, September quarter 2026 (2026-10-18)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZIER Quarterly Survey of Business Opinion, September quarter 2026 (2026-10-18).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#businessconditions #newzealand
First Impressions: NZIER Quarterly Survey of Business Opinion,
September quarter 2026
By Michael Gordon
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
08:35 October 06 2026 Share
Businesses are feeling more optimistic about the months ahead, but current conditions
remain tough for many industries. Cost pressures eased off a little compared to last quarter.
Key results (seasonally adjusted)
General business situation: +40 (Prev: +14)

### Page 2

Trading activity, past three months: -1 (Prev: +1) 
Trading activity, next three months: +15 (Prev: +11) 
Average selling prices, past three months: +27 (Prev: +37) 
Average selling prices, next three months: +45 (Prev: +51)

The NZIER’s quarterly survey of business opinion showed a strong lift in general confidence in
September, despite resurgent fuel prices and the uncertainty around the looming election. Firms were
more confident about their prospects for the next few months, even though their recent performance
remained rough in many industries. 
The survey was held over a relatively compressed period (9 to 28 September), with around 40% of
responses coming back on the first day. Later responses were less confident, as the resurgence in
world oil prices was finally reflected in prices at the pump over the course of the month. 
General business sentiment rose to a net 40% positive compared to a net 14% in June, returning to
around the levels seen in the December quarter last year (before the Middle East conflict). However,
the own-activity measures, which tend to have a closer correspondence with GDP growth, were more
subdued. Trading activity over the last three months was slightly weaker than in June, while
expectations for the quarter ahead saw a more modest four-point increase to +15%. 
Other forward-looking measures were also up for the quarter. Hiring intentions rose strongly to a net
23% positive (although past employment was down). Notably, firms continue to report that workers are
becoming harder to find, despite the ongoing rise in unemployment over the last couple of years. That
said, the proportion of firms citing labour as a factor limiting output declined this quarter. Investment
intentions picked up, more so for plant and machinery than for buildings. 
The results were mixed across industries. Manufacturing and services were more upbeat for the
months ahead but reported substantially weaker performance over the last quarter. In contrast, builders
and merchants reported both stronger past results and greater optimism about the near-term outlook. 
The pricing measures in the survey were down compared to June – not entirely surprising as fuel
prices were lower on average through the September quarter compared to the June quarter. The
notable exception was merchants, who expected both higher costs and prices for the quarter ahead. 
The implications for the RBNZ ahead of its 28 October policy review are unclear – there is certainly no
smoking gun in this report that would demand that the RBNZ abandon the “pause” strategy
communicated at the September MPS. Optimism among businesses remains high despite the obvious
headwinds, but there is still a wide gap between hopes and reality. The pricing measures have eased
compared to last quarter, but that may simply confirm the RBNZ’s expectation that annual inflation will
drop from the 4.1% recorded last quarter as fuel prices eased. The September quarter CPI, released
on 22 October, may be more informative in that respect. 
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist
P: +64 21 749 506

## Document 246: Genius Electronic(3406TW) Nomura 261005

Source PDF: all report pdf/Genius Electronic(3406TW) Nomura 261005.pdf

EQUITY: TECHNOLOGY
Genius Electronic Optical   3406.TW 3406 TT
Global Markets Research
5 October 2026
EBO and CPO an ideal leverage of its capabilities 
Quantifying EBO and CPO opportunities; reiterate Buy
Action: Maintain Buy; raise TP to TWD1,415, implying ~34% upside
We expect AI optical interconnect and communication opportunities (e.g. co-packaged optics 
fiber array unit [CPO FAU], expanded beam optical [EBO], micro multi-fiber connector [MMC]) 
to transform the traditional optical lens industry over the next five years, and believe 
Taiwanese handset camera lens makers (e.g. Largan [3008 TT, Buy] and Genius) possess 
inherent advantages to cater to these new markets, given their high-precision molding+plastic 
injection, optical design, active alignment, optical coating and optical inspection capabilities, as 
well as clean-room facilities and sufficient free cash flow from cash-cow handset lens 
businesses.
In addition to holding the position of Apple’s (AAPL US, Not rated) No. 2 lens supplier, we believe 
Genius has obtained 3M’s (MMM US, Not rated) EBO ferrule order and will start shipping by end-
2026F or early-2027F. Genius appears well-positioned to supply various CPO FAU 
components (lens, prism, V groove, FA, etc.) and may potentially handle FAU assembly if 
requested by customers. We estimate the EBO and CPO-related new businesses will 
contribute <3% and 16-20% earnings for Genius in 2027F and 2028F, respectively (details 
in Fig. 10). Yet, given the high dollar content and long-term growth potential of CPO/FAU related 
products over the next five years, we believe optical lens makers with inherent advantages in this 
new field deserve stock re-rating; we thus raise our target P/E to 25x (near the high end of the 
historical range of 10-30x since 2020), from 17x. We reiterate Buy, and raise our TP to 
TWD1,415, based on 25x 2028F EPS of TWD56.61 (vs. prior TP of TWD568, based on 17x 
2025F EPS of TWD33.4, set two years ago). Genius trades at 25x 2027F P/E.
Apple: Sunny’s market share loss and continued camera upgrades in 2027-28F
Sunny (2382 HK, Neutral) earlier faced some yield issues with the new variable aperture (VA) 
main camera lens for the iPhone 18 Pro series, and this could benefit Largan (more directly) and 
Genius (indirectly) in the competitive landscape. It remains uncertain whether Genius will be 
introduced as a third lens supplier to iPhone Pro’ main camera in the future, and this poses an 
upside risk to our forecasts. However, given the headwinds of rising memory costs and the 
potential cannibalization of the 18 Pro series by the iPhone Duo, we forecast slower iPhone lens 
shipments from end-2026F to 1H27F, before the 20th anniversary iPhone (Pro Max) and the 
second-gen iPhone Duo are introduced in 2H27F. We expect further upgrades to the iPhone Pro 
main camera in 2H27F, and introduction of a new periscope camera for the Pro Max in 2H28F.

Company profile
Genius is the second source in Apple's lenses, and is gaining share in recent years. VR is a new growth engine for the company, with Facebook and
Apple being its key VR customers.
Valuation Methodology
Our TP of TWD1,415 is based on 25x 2028F EPS of TWD56.6. Our target P/E of 25x is near the high end of Genius' historical range of 10-30x since
2020. The benchmark index for this stock is TAIEX.
Risks that may impede the achievement of the target price

Downside risks are: 1) worse-than-expected iPhone demand, 2) rising ASP pressure due to competition, 3) worse-than-expected progress in
market-share gain in iPhone rear cam lenses, and 4) slower-than-expected penetration into other Android customers.

The company does not disclose any ESG information. However, based on industry features, we believe Genius is not a labor-intensive corporation
and it should not cause severe environmental issues.

Nomura | Genius Electronic Optical 5 October 2026

### Page 4

Optical communication – an ideal leverage of 
lens makers’ capabilities
Optical interconnect – from MPO to MMC and EBO
Multi-fiber push-on (MPO) is the current standardized physical-contact optical 
interconnect architecture designed to aggregate high-density ribbon fibers (typically 8, 12, 
16, 24 or 32 cores per ferrule) into a single compact interface. MPO connectors leverage 
precise physical contact enforced by a high mechanical spring force (around 10-20N) and 
steel guide pins for alignment to achieve ultra-low insertion loss (0.20-0.35dB) across 
clean fiber end-faces.
The micro multi-fiber connector (MMC) is another proprietary physical-contact optical 
interconnect standard beyond MPO developed by US Conec (unlisted) for high-density 
AI interconnects to directly address the server faceplate and panel space constraints. The 
MMC introduces a very small form-factor (VSFF) multi-fiber design that utilizes a 
miniaturized TMT ferrule (patented by US Conec; Fig. 1 ), and according to US Conec, 
the width of the TMT ferrule is similar to that of conventional MT ferrule yet with ~30% 
smaller in thickness and ~50% shorter in length to shrink the physical footprint to roughly 
one-third the size of an MPO. The spatial reduction consequently unlocks up to a 3x 
density advantage in patch panel ports and transceiver faceplates compared to standard 
MPO footprints (Fig. 2 ). Meanwhile, the MMC delivers a crucial density jump while 
preserving high optical performance, offering low insertion loss and supporting up to 
250um pitch multi-fiber ribbon arrays (including 12-, 16-, 24-, and 32-fiber configurations) 
to ensure interoperability with MPO ecosystems. The MMC also improves operational 
serviceability through US Conec’s DirectConec push-pull latching mechanism to allow 
technicians to plus and unplug individual cables even at a highly populated panel without 
external retraction tools.
Fig. 1:  TMT ferrule vs. conventional MT ferrule

Source: US Conec, Nomura research
Fig. 2:  MMC has a smaller footprint than MPO to enable a 3X 
optical interconnect density

Source: US Conec, Nomura research
Although MPO and MMC are the most popular standards of optical fiber connectors 
nowadays, they both rely on direct physical contact between ferrules and thus require 
higher spring pressure and precision alignments when connecting the cables. Also, 
physical contacts leave MPO and MMC’s exposed optical interfaces inherently 
susceptible to wear and tear over repeating mating cycles as well as potential signal 
degradation from dust and debris contamination (and therefore needs frequent cleaning 
and inspection).
Expanded beam optical (EBO), a patented technology by 3M (MMM US, Not rated; 3M 
owns over 100 patents on it, with 50+ patents pending), reshapes optical interconnect 
technologies by addressing pre-mentioned challenges of MPO or MMC, since EBO 
replaces traditional physical-contact end-face matings with non-contact, lens-based 
beam expansion (Fig. 4 ). EBO utilizes integrated total internal reflection (TIR) mirrors 
within precision polymer ferrules. As the light exits an optical fiber core and enters the 
ferrule, it strikes the internal TIR mirror that collimates and expands light beams up to 10x 
their core diameter before travelling through an intentional, tiny air gap to the mating 
Nomura | Genius Electronic Optical 5 October 2026
4

### Page 5

connector. An identical, paired TIR mirror is integrated within the receiving ferrule to 
capture the expanded beam and focuses it back into the receiving fiber core. According to 
3M, the EBO ferrule is vapor-coated with a special anti-reflection coating that efficiently 
reduces the reflection of light beams (Fig. 5 ).
Fig. 3:  An EBO ferrule

Source: 3M, Nomura research
Fig. 4:  Connection of two EBO ferrules

Source: Rosenberger, Nomura research

Fig. 5:  A cross-section illustration of EBO

Source: 3M, EBO MSA, Nomura research

Although one of the underlying issues with EBO is a higher insertion loss (0.35-0.75dB), 
3M’s EBO connector system still provides a few advantages compared to traditional 
physical-contact connectors:
Physical-contact connectors require frequent inspection and cleaning cycle before 
mating, while EBO is nearly immune to airborne particle contamination thanks to the 
embedded lens system and no direct surface contacts. An easier handling with 
reduced maintenance efforts therefore significantly lowers the installation time (as 
much as by 85%, according to 3M) and labor cost.
•
EBO can ensure consistent signal integrity across thousands of mating cycles due 
to contact-less designs to avoid face-wear and micro-scratching. By contrast, physical 
connectors grind against each other and ultimately lacks long-term durability.
•
Traditional connectors require precise fiber-to-fiber alignments, whereas the EBO 
connector system can be used in a hermaphroditic condition (socket/connector 
agnostic) given identical connector at both ends. As such, the risk of installation 
error is largely eliminated, even without the need for fiber-optic specialists.
•
EBO only demand a fraction (e.g. one-tenth) of the high spring pressure that 
compresses glass end-faces together in physical connectors, thereby minimizing 
•
Nomura | Genius Electronic Optical 5 October 2026
5

### Page 6

mechanical strains on chassis frames (demo video by 3M ). 
The contact-less, high density, blind mating, and low spring pressure features 
make EBO potentially an ideal optical connector for optical back-plane interconnect 
applications (similar to the function of orthogonal backplane or mid-plane in nVidia’s 
[NVDA US, Not rated] Kyber architecture), in our view.  
•
Fig. 6:  3M's current EBO ferrule offerings (12F/16F)

The EBO Multi-Source Agreement (EBO MSA) was established in March 2026 to turn 
3M’s proprietary EBO technology into an open, industry-wide standard for AI cluster 
infrastructure. The primary objective is to standardize EBO connector mechanical form 
factors, latching mechanisms, and optical fiber interface dimensions to allow hyperscalers 
to source interchangeable EBO connectors from multiple vendors rather than relying on 
one single supplier. According to EBO MSA, there are 9 end-user companies (including 
Microsoft [MSFT US, Not rated], Meta [META US, Not rated], nVidia, AMD [AMD US, Not 
rated], etc.) and 48 vendors in the membership by the end of September 2026 (Fig. 7 ). 
Members vote on specific connectors to develop and contribute design specifications – 
there are 18 types under discussion, and four are on the roadmap:
128f (8x16f) design specification is underway.•
16f MPO design is under discussion on specification.•
16f VSFF and external laser small form factor pluggable (ELSFP) are planned, and 
we note that EBO development for ELSFP could be challenging since high-power 
laser could damage the AR coating and polymer ferrules.
•

Who are the potential adopters of EBO connectors?
Of course, all the key members of EBO MSA are potential users of EBO connectors. But 
Nomura | Genius Electronic Optical 5 October 2026
6

### Page 7

for more concrete industry developments, 3M and Microsoft announced strategic
partnership in July 2026 (link ), and Microsoft was the first announced hyperscale cloud
provider to deploy 3M EBO technology in its Azure cloud. Microsoft’s early use of EBO
technology has shown the potential to reduce network deployment timelines in certain
environments. The technology has also demonstrated strong signal performance in live
data center conditions, where dust exposure and routine handling are typical during

installation and maintenance. The technology will help Microsoft reduce the need for
frequent cleaning and inspection while supporting reliable optical performance in dense,
high-volume deployment environments.
Meta joined EBO MSA in May 2026, and from our industry checks, we think Meta is likely
evaluating EBO technology for future ASIC mass-volume optical interconnect demand.
We also observe that AWS (AMZN US, Not rated), although it is not yet an MSA member,

could potentially evaluate this technology for its future ASIC mass-volume optical
interconnect demand.
Why optical plastic lens companies have advantages in
producing ferrules for passive optical connectors?
From MPO to MMC, the fiber density increases to 3x, and we notice that the precision
level requirements for MMC TMT ferrules also increase substantially versus MPO MT
ferrules. The high precision levels (<5um, or even <3um) of MMC TMT ferrules

usually exceed the traditional optical connector makers’ plastic injection capability
(usually >10um) and traditional connector makers need to invest new, higher precision
level tools to deal with those higher precision requirements. However, those precision
levels are about the right levels (or even lower-end than) which plastic camera lens
makers can do without much new investment in machine tools. This is why we think
plastic lens makers have advantage in the advanced passive optical connectors. The

reason why we do not see many lens makers enter MMC ferrule market at this moment is
because it is a patented technology by US Conec, and US Conec only licenses to limited
suppliers, such as Sumitomo (5802 JP, Buy), etc.
For EBO as well, although EBO ferrule looks complex in design and shape, it is a suitable
product for plastic camera lens makers to do. We estimate the difficulty and price of
EBO ferrule is around the levels of mid-range handset camera lenses.

3M likely outsources EBO ferrule to Genius
From our industry survey, 3M will likely outsource the EBO ferrule components to Genius,
which is done by high-precision plastic injection. Genius can leverage its handset camera
lens equipment and make new molds to produce the product. Hence, we do not expect
too much extra capex is required.
In our understanding, fiber connector makers can produce EBO after getting authorized
by 3M. We suspect that 3M will supply the key EBO ferrule to the authorized

manufacturers at least in the early stage of technology development. This may explain
why 3M comes to reach handset lens makers for the ferrule production.
Our base assumption for Genius’ EBO ferrule revenue contribution is about 2-3%
for 2027F and 2028F, by assuming an ASP of USD0.5-1 with 20-35mn units annual
volume. We assume the GM for this new product to be higher than corporate average.
The upside of EBO market will depend on the adoption and penetration of the technology

into hyperscalers’ AI optical interconnect roadmap. By our estimate, every rack
(containing 60-80 units of GPUs or AI ASICs), if adopting EBO for optical scale-up for
intra-rack or cross-rack interconnect technology, will potentially require 600-1,000
units of EBO ferrule components (300-500 pairs). Our base volume assumption for
Genius’ EBO shipments require one major CSP to adopt EBO in one major AI
platform, plus global sampling, yield loss, trial run demand and necessary

inventory buffer. If there are more than one major AI projects adopt EBO in 2028F, it will
represent potential upside to our forecasts.
CPO opportunities could be bigger than EBO, but less clear at
this moment
Unlike that EBO ferrule is a confirmed new business for Genius (though the market TAM
upside is unclear at this moment), CPO opportunities to Genius remain uncertain in
details, such as what products to offer, what are the business models, and who are

intermediate customers and/or end customers. Not to mention, the ramp-up of CPO
Nomura | Genius Electronic Optical 5 October 2026
7

### Page 8

market itself also has high uncertainties.
We believe Genius is working with FOCI (3363 TT, Not rated) in supplying key 
components for CPO FAU, such as V groove, prism, and/or fiber array (FA). It is also 
likely in talk with other FAU makers for various component supply (including lens, V 
groove, prism, FA, etc.). These FAU makers tend to do FAU final assembly in-house, and 
hence, may treat Genius as a component supplier to fill its internal capability or 
outsourcing partners’ gap. Genius’ flexibility in various component offering for CPO FAU 
could make it an easier partner for FAU customers, versus Largan (it tends to do more FA 
or FAU, rather than just components), and may see revenue contribution earlier than 
Largan. However, we assume USD25-35 ASP for Genius’ CPO FAU component offering 
in our base assumptions, given that it may supply more components rather than doing 
finished FAU products (ASP USD>100).
In the other side, we also see opportunities for Genius to get FAU module business from 
customers like Corning (GLW US, Not rated), but the progress of Genius to do such full 
assembly lines could be slower than Largan, as Genius still relies more on external 
equipment supply, unlike Largan having strong internal equipment design and 
manufacturing capability.
We assume CPO FAU to contribute Genius’ EPS by <1% in 2027F and 17% in 2028F.

Definition of Nomura Group's equity research rating system and sectors
The rating system is a relative system, indicating expected performance against a specific benchmark identified for each individual stock, subject to 
limited management discretion. An analyst’s target price is an assessment of the current intrinsic fair value of the stock based on an appropriate 
valuation methodology determined by the analyst. Valuation methodologies include, but are not limited to, discounted cash flow analysis, expected 
return on equity and multiple analysis. Analysts may also indicate expected absolute upside/downside relative to the stated target price, defined as 
Nomura | Genius Electronic Optical 5 October 2026

### Page 13

(target price - current price)/current price.

SECTORS
A 'Bullish' stance, indicates that the analyst expects the sector to outperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Neutral' stance, 
indicates that the analyst expects the sector to perform in line with the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Bearish' stance, indicates that the 
analyst expects the sector to underperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. Sectors that are labelled as 'Not rated' or shown as 'N/A' are 
not assigned ratings. Benchmarks are as follows: United States: S&P 500; Europe: Dow Jones STOXX 600; Global Emerging Markets (ex-Asia): 
MSCI Emerging Markets ex-Asia. Japan/Asia ex-Japan: Sector ratings are not assigned.

Target Price
A Target Price, if discussed, indicates the analyst’s forecast for the share price with a 12-month time horizon, reflecting in part the analyst's 
estimates for the company's earnings. The achievement of any target price may be impeded by general market and macroeconomic trends, and by 
other risks related to the company or the market, and may not occur if the company's earnings differ from estimates.

### Page 2

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2. 2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%) . 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.4% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4,25% - 4,50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth slowed to 4.3% y/y in Q2 2026 vs. 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth 
remains characterized by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports and the fragility of sectors that rely 
on domestic demand has widened this year. Domestic demand has weakened significantly in recent months. Retail sales have barely increased, 
the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting investment 
and private consumption will increase over the last months of the year . CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

EUROZONE 
Eurozone growth is expected to slow to 1.1% in 2026 (from 1.3% in 2025), before strengthening to 1.5% in 2027, supported by higher defence 
and infrastructure spending as well as the global AI capex cycle. The economy has proven resilient to the energy shock, notab ly thanks to the 
increasingly tangible impact of G erman defence spending on the real economy. Elevated energy prices in the autumn (especially for natural 
gas), combined with resilient growth, are set to accelerate inflation to a peak around the turn of the year. Annual average i nflation is thus 
expected to reach  3.1% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), before easing to 3.0% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, we 
anticipate another 25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause . That said, 
stronger than expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

### Page 3

FRANCE 
GDP growth remained low in Q2 (0% after -0.2% in Q1) as a result of deteriorated agricultural and construction output, as well as higher 
inflation (2.5% in 2026 according to our forecast, after 0.9% in 2025) . In 2026, GDP growth should  reach 0.5% (after 0.9% in 2025), driven 
mainly by exports.

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would improve slightly to 1.3% in 2026 and remain at this level in 2027  (1.2% in 2025). The economy has so far weathered 
both the energy price shock and domestic political volatility. That said, growth is expected to decelerate in the second half  of the year, while 
inflation is set to remain stubbornly above target (averaging 3.2% in both 2026 and 2027), driven by higher energy prices and mounting second-
round effects. In response, we expect the BoE to deliver a single insurance hike in November 2026, bringing the Bank Rate to 4.00%. The BoE 
is then expected to resume easing in H2 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank Rate to 3.50% by end-year.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 1.0% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.2% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy  and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of +2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end -2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.

auto volumes fell 3%, while utilities surged 3.5% MoM as output is
ramped up to meet demand from data centres and expanding
manufacturers. Mining was up only 0.2% MoM.

3
• 升息預期推高公債及信用債殖利率，但升息落地後有趨穩現象：早在聯準會利率會議召開前夕，市場不斷出現
升息聲浪，推高10年期公債利率一度站上5%，壓抑9月以來信用債市表現，尤其以投資級債表現最為弱勢，也
拖累全年總報酬率轉負 ，但利差走勢其實相當平穩 ，８月以來美投資級債利差大致在 75~85基點區間震盪 。
FOMC會議後公債利率略自高檔回落，並沒有因為升息而持續創高，信用債市殖利率也趨於穩定。
• 股市情緒受到影響但未失控 ：9月以來股市情緒受到一定程度影響 ，不過並未出現恐慌情緒 ，僅利率敏感性類
股震盪較大，非核心消費之外的類股年至今仍多收正報酬。聯準會升息落地後，股市單日跌幅也可控。

風險管理式升息，而非典型的升息循環重新啟動
2026年底前預期還有一次升息
貨幣政策
通膨廣度其實已有所收斂，預期此一趨勢將延續
4
• 風險管理式升息 ，而非典型的升息循環重新啟動 ：9月FOMC會議全票通過升息一碼至 3.75%-4.0%，這是自
2023年7月以來的首次升息。本次會議的重點可歸納如下：(1)利率預估點陣圖顯示至2026年底前還有一碼的升息
空間；(2)上調通膨與經濟成長預估值，同時下調失業率，是一個很鷹派的組合，不過調整幅度其實很小，2026
年的整體PCE通膨和核心PCE通膨僅上調0.1個百分點；(3)判斷本次會議並非典型的升息循環重新啟動，而是「不
承諾路徑的風險管理式升息」。
• 年底前再升息一碼後進入觀察期 ：搭配利率預估點陣圖，以及本行對2027年通膨走勢的判斷(8月的CPI數據已
經看到通膨廣度已經有收斂態勢，搭配本次升息，預期此一趨勢將延續，2Q27通膨有望降至2.5%以下)，本行
基準情境認為年底前再升息一碼後，2027上半年進入按兵不動的觀察期，2027年下半年有望轉向寬鬆立場。

美債利率
美債殖利率通常要等確認升息到頂後才會回落
5
• 聯準會升息證明公信力，美債市場大致正向表現：9月FOMC會議前市場已先定價升息，而使得美國10年期公債
殖利率來到5%以上，而在FOMC會議後短率上揚、長率大致穩定，顯示市場主要重新定價近期政策變動，而非同
步大幅上修長期通膨或長期利率風險，是一項相對正面的訊號。
• 推演殖利率4Q26偏高表現、1H27適度回落：根據歷史經驗，美債殖利率通常要等確認升息到頂後才會回落，
4Q26在鷹派論調+期中選舉+高油價的影響下，研判美國10年期公債殖利率維持偏高水位 。2027年隨通膨放
緩，市場確認停止升息後，美債殖利率將適度回落。預估未來兩季美國10年期公債殖利率的區間將處於4.5%-
5.1%，美國30年期公債殖利率則在 5.1%-5.5%之間。尾端風險是「後續的升息不斷超過市場預期 」，不過在
油價不持續失控向上的情況下，尾端風險的機率並不高。
資料來源：左， Bloomberg ，2026/9/17，右，3FOURTEEN，2026/9。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9/17

受惠戰爭
• 景氣擴張週期尚未結束 ，升息對股市影響有限 ：9月FOMC決議升息一碼，但股市反應相對有限 。一方面，市
場已提前消化升息預期；更重要的是，過去一年全球景氣維持穩定擴張，且前景仍偏正向。回顧過往景氣擴張
階段的升息循環，市場往往在升息前因政策不確定性而較為震盪；然而 ，一旦後續經濟數據確認景氣仍處於擴
張軌道，即使估值面受到利率壓抑，企業獲利成長仍有望成為推動股市續漲的主要動能。
• 估值壓縮，選股回到獲利能見度：在股市策略方面，現階段獲利成長動能最強的仍是受惠於 AI長期趨勢的科技
供應鏈。部分科技股已於7至8月率先修正整理，不僅評價壓力有所緩解，也提供較具吸引力的布局機會。不過，
中東地緣衝突尚未落幕，加上國際油價維持高檔，使國防、能源等受惠地緣局勢的族群，成為兼具主題性與避
險特性的配置選擇。整體而言，在聯準會重啟升息的環境下，上半年資金雨露均霑的普漲行情可能逐漸降溫，

但獲利能見度高、基本面持續改善的族群，仍可望獲得資金青睞並展現相對優勢。

資料來源：(左) Fed，2026/9/17；(右) ICE BofA indices，2026/9/16 。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9/17
• 基本面穩健態勢不變，有助穩定信用債利差：FOMC會議證實美國經濟韌性，在景氣成長及企業獲利正向循環
未結束前，穩健債信將支持信用債利差走勢持穩，雖然目前美國公債利率已大致反應升息預期，但政策不確定
性仍牽動短線較大波動，利率敏感度較低的非投資級債預期將有相對突出的表現 ，而高利率及高債信品質的族
群則可長期持有以息收為主，而外幣債與美國雙政策連動性較低，新當債與澳幣債走勢可望回歸自身貨幣政策。
• 貨幣政策路徑上修帶動美元走強：美元已從2026年初的降息交易轉向受到美伊戰爭、油價上漲及通膨預期升溫
支撐，但此波強勢更多來自地緣政治與高利率環境 ，而非美國基本面出現結構性轉強 。Fed本次升息後美元指
數當日上升約0.6%，美元指數再度回測100。從聯準會官員利率點陣圖判斷，短期利率路徑較先前預期偏高，

支撐美元表現；惟在通膨回穩後 ，貨幣政策仍有機會重新轉向較寬鬆立場 。留意4Q26共和黨失去絕對多數後
對財政樽節的預期，美元短線路徑轉強。美元指數目標價從97上修至99。

[インド]～９月インフレ率は２年弱ぶりの高い伸びに、コアインフレ率も加速するなどインフレ圧力が強まる～ 
12 日に発表された９月の消費者物価は前年同月比＋5.54％となり、 前月 （同＋4.82％） から加速
して 2024 年10 月以来の高い伸びとなった。前月比は＋0.69％と前月（同＋0.73％）からわずかに
上昇ペースが鈍化したものの、 原油価格が再び上昇していることを反映してエネルギー価格の上昇
が続いているほか、 生鮮品をはじめとする食料品価格も上昇しており、 生活必需品を中心とするイ
ンフレが続いている。食料品とエネルギーを除いたコアインフレ率は前年同月比＋4.33％となり、
前月（同＋4.16％）から加速した。前月比は＋0.40％と前月（同＋0.54％）から上昇ペースが鈍化
したものの、 エネルギー価格の上昇に伴う輸送コストの増加に加え、 金融市場でのルピー安による
輸入インフレも重なり、幅広い財で価格が上昇している。さらに、サービス物価も上昇基調で推移
しており、インフレ圧力が広がっている様子がうかがえる。

### Page 2

2/4

[オーストラリア]～大都市部を中心に雇用に変調、労働市場への参入意欲にも陰りの兆し～ 
15 日に発表された９月の失業率 （季調済） は4.7％となり、 前月 （4.7％） から２ヵ月連続で横ば
いとなった。失業者数は前月比＋0.9 万人と前月（同＋2.8 万人）から４ヵ月連続で増加した。雇
用形態別では、パートタイムの求職者数（同▲0.9 万人）が２ヵ月ぶりの減少に転じる一方、フル
タイムの求職者数 （同＋1.8 万人） は４ヵ月連続で増加しており、 失業者数全体を押し上げている。
一方、雇用者数は前月比▲3.9 万人と前月（同＋2.9 万人）から２ヵ月ぶりの減少に転じた。雇用
形態別では、フルタイムの雇用者数（同＋3.4 万人）が２ヵ月ぶりの増加に転じる一方、パートタ
イムの雇用者数（同▲7.4 万人）が減少に転じ、雇用全体を押し下げた。地域別では、資源関連産
業が集積する西オーストラリア州で雇用の増加基調が続いているものの、 最大都市シドニーを擁す
るニュー・サウス・ウェールズ州や、第２の都市メルボルンを擁するヴィクトリア州では雇用が減
少するなど、大都市部を中心に雇用環境に変化の兆しがみられる。さらに、労働力人口は前月比▲
3.0 万人と前月 （同＋5.7 万人） から２ヵ月ぶりの減少に転じた。 労働参加率も66.8％と前月 （67.0％）
から 0.2pt 低下しており、 雇用環境の悪化を背景に、 労働市場への参入意欲にも陰りが出つつある
様子がうかがえる。

[韓国]～高齢層で雇用に頭打ちの一方、若年層に持ち直しの動き、雇用環境に変化の兆し～ 
16 日に発表された９月の失業率 （季調済） は2.6％となり、 前月 （2.7％） から0.1pt 改善した。
失業者数は前月比▲4.6 万人と前月（同▲3.3 万人）から２ヵ月連続で減少しており、年代別では
20 代や 30 代のほか、50 代や 60 代以上など幅広い年代で減少する動きがみられる。一方、雇用者

代といった若年層のほか、30 代でも増加ペースが加速しているうえ、60 代以上でも増加が続いて
いる。雇用形態別では、正規雇用者を中心に増加する一方、非正規雇用者は減少するなど、対照的
な動きがみられる。こうしたなか、労働力人口は前月比▲0.1 万人と前月（同＋0.5 万人）から４

動きがみられ、 中高年層の一部で労働市場からの退出が進んでいる可能性がうかがえる。 その一方、
これまで厳しい状況が続いてきた10 代や20 代など若年層の雇用環境には持ち直しの動きがみられ
ており、年代によって雇用情勢に違いが現れつつある。

[シンガポール]～世界的な AI・半導体需要の旺盛さが景気と外需を押し上げる展開が続く～ 
14 日に発表された７-９月の実質ＧＤＰ成長率 （速報値） は前年同期比＋5.4％となり、 前期 （同
＋5.9％） から伸びが鈍化した。 前期比年率ベースの成長率も＋4.42％と前期 （同＋6.15％） から鈍
化したものの、引き続きプラス成長を維持するなど、景気は堅調に推移している。分野別の動きを
みると、 世界的なＡＩ （人工知能） ・半導体関連需要の旺盛さを反映して、 電子部品関連のほか、 精
密機械関連や一般機械関連の生産は堅調に推移した。 一方、 化学製品関連や医薬品関連の生産低迷
が重しとなり、製造業全体の生産は伸び悩んだ。また、ＡＩ・半導体関連需要の旺盛さを受けた設
備投資やデータセンター関連投資の活発化に加え、 公共投資の進捗を反映して、 建設業の生産は上
振れしている。さらに、小売・卸売関連のほか、運輸・貿易関連、金融関連など幅広いサービス業
で生産が拡大しており、景気拡大を下支えしている様子がうかがえる。 
16 日に発表された９月の非石油輸出額は前年同月比＋68.4％となり、 前月 （同＋46.1％） から伸
びが加速した。前月比も＋25.53％と前月（同＋10.78％）から２ヵ月連続で拡大しており、持ち直
しの動きを強めている。 化学製品関連や医薬品関連のほか、 食料品関連の輸出に下押し圧力がかか
ったものの、 半導体など電子部品関連や精密機械関連のほか、 鉄鋼製品関連や鉱物資源関連の輸出
は旺盛に推移している。 原油関連を含む総輸出額も前年同月比＋51.5％となり、 前月 （同＋49.2％）
から伸びが加速した。前月比も＋5.1％と前月（同＋3.1％）から４ヵ月連続で拡大しており、単月
ベースの輸出額は過去最高を更新した。 一方、輸入額は前年同月比＋49.9％となり、前月（同＋
39.3％） から伸びが加速した。 前月比も＋12.4％と前月 （同▲0.3％） から２ヵ月ぶりの拡大に転じ
ており、 輸出と同様に持ち直しの動きをみせている。 輸出の堅調さを背景に、 設備機械関連や素材 ・

Europe Trends

2026 年 10 月１日

1/5 
フランス大統領選、迫る「ルペン対メランション」 
～現実味を増す極右大統領の誕生～ 
 経済調査部 首席エコノミスト  田中 理 
（℡：050-5474-7494）

（要旨） 
○ 2027 年春のフランス大統領選で、決選投票が極右政党・国民連合（ＲＮ）のルペン氏と、極
左政党・不服従のフランス（ＬＦＩ）メランション氏の対決となる可能性を示す世論調査が
相次いでいる。最新調査では、中道候補をフィリップ元首相に一本化しても、メランション
氏が上回ることが示された。マクロン政権への逆風や財政再建に伴う負担への反発、メラン
ション氏の固定的な支持基盤、社会民主派の独自候補が、中道勢力の決選投票進出を難しく
している。

■中道一本化でもメランション氏が２位 
来年春のフランス大統領選挙に向けて、極右政党・国民連合（ＲＮ）のルペン氏に次ぐ二番手
争いが混沌としてきた。初回投票で過半数を獲得する候補がいない場合、上位２名が決選投票に
進出し、その勝者が大統領に選出される。各種の世論調査では、ルペン氏が圧倒的な支持を集め
てリードするなか、極左政党・不服従のフランス（ＬＦＩ）のメランション氏と中道政党・地平
線（Horizons）を率いるフィリップ元首相が熾烈な二番手争いを繰り広げている。 
メランション氏とフィリップ氏の支持率は、他の候補者の組み合わせ次第で変わる。これまで
の調査では、フィリップ氏とは所属政党が異なるが、同じ中道勢力としてマクロン大統領を支え
てきた中道政党・ルネサンス（Renaissance）のアタル元首相が同時に出馬する場合、メランショ
ン氏が両中道候補を振り切り、二番手になるものが多かった。現地メディアでは、中道票の分散
を避けるため、来年早々にも中道勢力が候補者を一本化するとの見方が広がっていた。フィリッ
プ氏に比べて劣勢のアタル氏が出馬を断念する場合、フィリップ氏がメランション氏を僅かに交
わし、決選投票に滑り込むとみられていた。その場合の決選投票の組み合わせは、ルペン氏とフ
ィリップ氏となる。 
ところが最近の世論調査では、アタル氏が不出馬の場合も、メランション氏の支持がフィリッ
プ氏を上回るものが増えている。９月 28 日に公表されたHarris Interactiveの調査では、アタル
氏が出馬を断念する場合でも、メランション氏の支持が18％と、フィリップ氏の16％を上回る
（図表１） 。この場合、決選投票でルペン氏に対峙するのは、フィリップ氏ではなく、メランショ
ン氏となる。

### Page 2

2/5

決選投票の組み合わせ別の世論調査では、ルペン氏がメランション氏と対峙した場合に圧勝が
予想される一方、フィリップ氏と対峙した場合には接戦が予想される（図表２） 。最近では、フィ
リップ氏もルペン氏と対峙した場合に劣勢となりつつある。決選投票の組み合わせが「極右対極
左」となった場合、ルペン氏の大統領就任が現実味を増す。二番手争いを繰り広げるフィリップ
氏とメランション氏の支持率の差は僅かで、選挙まで半年以上を残す現時点で、両者の優劣が確
定したとみることは勿論できない。ただ、従来想定されてきた「極右対中道」の決選投票が当然
視できなくなっていることは確かだ。

注：調査期間は2026年9月22～24日

3/5

■フィリップ氏を苦しめるマクロン後継者のイメージ 
 これまでフィリップ氏の支持率の伸び悩みは、アタル氏など複数の候補が中道票を奪い合って
いることが一因とされてきた。実際、アタル氏が出馬しない中道候補が一本化されるケースで
は、一本化されない場合と比べてフィリップ氏の支持率が上昇する。しかし、最新の調査が示し
ているのは、それだけではメランション氏を上回れない可能性である。背景には、中道勢力がマ
クロン大統領の後継を巡って分裂していることに加え、フィリップ氏自身にマクロン大統領の後
継者としてのイメージが残っていることがある。フィリップ氏は2017～20 年にマクロン大統領の
下で首相を務めた。現在は自身の中道右派政党を立ち上げ、マクロン大統領から一定の距離を取
っているものの、これまで政権を支えていた中道勢力の中心人物であり、多くの有権者からマク
ロン路線を継承する候補とみなされる。 
フィリップ氏の掲げる政策は財政再建や経済改革を重視するものが多い。９月 29 日には、年金
出所：各種世論調査資料より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所が作成

### Page 4

4/5 
財政の立て直しに向けて、年金支給開始年齢を段階的に65 歳に引き上げ、満額受給に必要な拠出
期間を 45 年に延ばす改革案を公表した。巨額の財政赤字と政府債務を抱えるフランスにとって、
財政再建は避けて通れない課題だが、年金改革をはじめ国民負担を伴う政策は選挙で支持を広げ
るうえで難しさを伴う。 
2027 年の予算審議が本格化するのを控え、議会では財政赤字の削減に必要な歳出抑制策や増税
策を巡って、主要政党間の対立が深まっている。政府はこのままでは2026 年の財政赤字が国内総
生産（ＧＤＰ）比で5.4％に達するとの見通しを示し、2027 年に赤字を５％程度に抑えるために
は巨額の財政改善が必要としている（詳しくは９月 24 日付けレポート「難航するフランスの予算
協議」を参照されたい）。歳出削減や増税への反発をどう抑えるかが、大統領選挙を前に与党の大
きな政治課題となっている。フィリップ氏が財政規律を前面に出すほど、現在の予算協議で表面
化している財政再建の必要性と国民負担への反発という難題を引き受けることになる。

■メランション氏を支える固定票 
これとは対照的に、メランション氏には対立候補の組み合わせに左右されにくい強固な支持基
盤がある。メランション氏は最近の複数の世論調査で15～20％程度の支持を確保している。特に
若年層や低所得層から高い支持を集めている。他の陣営が分裂するなかで固定票を集めることが
できれば、初回投票では大きな強みとなる。 
二番手争いをさらに複雑にするのが、中道左派の社会民主勢力の動きである。かつて二大政党
の一角を占めた社会党（ＰＳ）を中心とする同勢力は、メランション氏から距離を置き、独自候
補の擁立を目指し、10 月に候補者を一本化する予備選を予定している。社会党のフォール書記長
などに加えて、2024 年の欧州議会選挙で社会民主勢力を復権に導いたグリュックスマン氏などが
参加する。独自の中道左派政党「公共の広場（Place publique） 」を率いるグリュックスマン氏
は、2024 年の欧州議会選挙を社会党などともに戦い、マクロン大統領の中道勢力に迫る支持を集
めた。同氏はメランション氏と明確な距離を置き、親ＥＵ・ウクライナ支援・再工業化を軸とす
る欧州主義を掲げ、財政・社会保障政策では社会党の主流派よりもやや改革志向の立場を取る。
富裕層や高額相続への負担強化を支持する一方、企業資産への一律課税には慎重で、企業活動へ
の影響を配慮する。グリュックスマン氏が社会民主勢力の統一候補となった場合、メランション
氏より穏健な左派票を取り込む一方、中道のフィリップ氏への票の流入を抑える可能性がある。

■財政問題は大統領選挙にも影響する 
フィリップ氏にとって今後の課題は、アタル氏など中道勢力内の競合候補との争いを勝ち抜く
ことだけではない。親ＥＵ・財政規律・経済改革を支持する有権者を固めるとともに、中道左派
や穏健保守層にまで支持を広げられるかが焦点となる。 
さらに注目されるのが、2027 年予算を巡る政局と大統領選との連動である。足元でフランス国
債の利回りが上昇しており、財政問題は金融市場の重要な関心事となっている。政府は巨額の財
政赤字を削減する必要がある一方、大統領選を控えて大規模な歳出削減や増税を打ち出せば、有

### Page 5

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
5/5 
権者の反発を招く恐れがある。このジレンマは、財政再建を政策の柱とするフィリップ氏にとっ
て特に大きい。政府が財政規律を優先すれば、自身の主張との整合性は高まるが、緊縮措置に対
する反発を受けやすい。政府が財政再建を先送りすれば、フィリップ氏は大統領選でより厳しい
改革の必要性を訴えざるを得なくなる。 
 2027 年大統領選までまだ半年以上あり、現在の世論調査から決選投票の顔ぶれを断定すること
はできない。ただ、最近の調査が示した重大な変化は、中道候補をフィリップ氏に一本化すれば
自動的に決選投票に進めるという従来のシナリオが揺らぎ始めたことである。マクロン大統領退
任後の中道勢力を誰がまとめるのか、財政再建を巡る国民負担への反発をどう乗り越えるのか、
社会民主派の候補がどこまで支持を集めるのか、そしてメランション氏が現在の支持基盤を維持
できるのか、混戦となった二番手争いの行方に注目が集まる。

以 上
